fetchPriority="high" alt="Digital creator producing one original image while duplicated versions fade beside an abstract X-shaped light" class="h-auto w-full object-cover" decoding="async"/></picture></div></figure></div><div data-toc-content="true"><div class="payload-richtext max-w-none mx-auto prose md:prose-md dark:prose-invert techi-post-content"><p>X is replacing Creator Revenue Sharing with a program that rewards eligible original posts according to qualified audience impressions. Under <a href="https://help.x.com/en/using-x/original-content-rewards" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Original Content Rewards</a>, those impressions accrue only when Premium users see a post in the Home Timeline. The policy makes originality an eligibility rule while reserving direct payout value for the platform&#x27;s paying audience.</p><p>The change arrives with a firm handoff. X stopped accepting new Revenue Sharing enrollments on August 7, 2026, will retire the old program on September 7, and plans to begin letting existing members apply to the replacement on September 8. That month-long transition leaves creators with two separate questions: whether their work is original enough for X, and whether the right accounts are seeing it.</p><section id="key-takeaways" role="note" data-nosnippet="true" data-no-article-callout="true" aria-labelledby="key-takeaways-title" class="techi-key-takeaways not-prose relative my-6 overflow-hidden rounded-lg border class="mt-0.5 flex h-6 w-6 select-none items-center justify-center rounded-[7px] bg-techi-teal-300/15 font-mono text-[10px] font-black tabular-nums text-techi-teal-100 ring-1 cannot.</span></span></li><li class="!mb-0 grid min-w-0 grid-cols-[1.55rem_minmax(0,1fr)] items-start gap-2 rounded-lg border border-white/10 bg-white/[0.045] p-2.5 text-[13px] leading-5 text-slate-200"><span aria-hidden="true" class="mt-0.5 flex h-6 w-6 select-none items-center justify-center rounded-[7px] bg-techi-teal-300/15 font-mono text-[10px] font-black tabular-nums text-techi-teal-100 ring-1 ring-techi-teal-300/20">04</span><span class="min-w-0"><strong class="inline font-bold tracking-tight text-white">The economic filter</strong><span aria-hidden="true" class="mx-1 text-slate-500">-</span><span class="text-slate-200">Payouts count qualified Home Timeline impressions from Premium users, while X does not publish a fixed rate per thousand views.</span></span></li></ol></section><h2>This is a new application, not a renamed account setting</h2><p>Current Revenue Sharing members do not roll over automatically. X says they will keep earning through September 7 and receive scheduled payouts on August 14 and August 28, followed by a final payment for eligible earnings accrued through the retirement date, expected around September 11. The <a href="https://help.x.com/en/using-x/creator-revenue-sharing" target="_blank" rel="noopener">retirement notice</a> directs those creators to apply for the new program when access begins rolling out.</p><p>The replacement also changes the headline entry metric. Applicants need at least 500,000 Home Timeline impressions from verified users during the previous 90 days, at least 500 verified followers, an active Premium, Premium+ or Premium Business subscription, and an account in good standing. They must also be at least 18, live in a supported country, and apply through an eligible personal or business account; accounts belonging to political or government organizations are excluded. Replies do not count toward the 500,000-impression requirement. X says applications should receive a decision within three business days, but meeting the listed conditions does not guarantee admission.</p><p>The old program required 5 million organic impressions over three months. It is tempting to describe 500,000 as a 90% cut in the bar, but the two numbers are not equivalent. The new threshold is tied to verified-user Home Timeline exposure and excludes reply impressions. A creator can have a much larger public reach and still miss the narrower qualifying metric.</p><p>That distinction matters because X has spent years widening the formats creators can publish, including its <a href="/x-rolling-out-4k-video-feature-for-creators/">4K video rollout for creators</a>. Original Content Rewards says text posts, Articles, videos and images can all earn. Every major format is eligible, but payout credit still depends on distribution to a specific audience.</p><h2>X drew a line between commentary and aggregation</h2><p>X&#x27;s definition of originality is more practical than a blanket ban on using other people&#x27;s material. Work that a creator writes, films, designs or produces qualifies. So can reaction, analysis and reporting that adds a genuine perspective or context. The policy recognizes that much of X&#x27;s value comes from people interpreting events, not only uploading material created in isolation.</p><p>The exclusions target a familiar set of high-volume tactics: copying a post, downloading and reuploading someone else&#x27;s media, changing a few words, adding a filter or text overlay, and compiling other creators&#x27; work without substantial new framing. Cross-platform reposts are ineligible when the person posting them is not the original creator. X is telling clipping and aggregation accounts that speed and reach alone no longer create payable value.</p><p>There is an important legal boundary in the policy. Original Content Rewards is an eligibility test, not a copyright license. X says a transformed post could satisfy the rewards definition and still infringe intellectual property rights. The creator remains responsible for permissions. In other words, “original enough to earn” and “lawful to publish” are separate judgments.</p><p>The move fits a wider effort to extract more revenue and distinct content from the same network. <a href="/x-launches-marketplace-inactive-usernames/">X&#x27;s marketplace for inactive usernames</a> monetized scarce identity. Original Content Rewards applies a similar scarcity logic to posts: copied supply is abundant, while material associated with a recognizable voice is harder to replace.</p><h2>Premium impressions are the economic center</h2><p>For payouts, X defines a qualified impression as a unique view from a Premium Basic, Premium, Premium+ or Premium Business user in the Home Timeline, with at least half of the post visible. Repeat views from the same account on the same post do not add another qualified impression. Paid, promoted, fraudulent and artificially generated views are excluded.</p><p>This means a creator&#x27;s total view counter and payable audience can diverge sharply. A post can travel among non-paying users, appear in replies or circulate through routes outside the Home Timeline without those views becoming qualified impressions. Public reach may still help a post spread, but X has reserved direct payout value for attention coming from subscribers.</p><p>That arrangement aligns the program with X&#x27;s subscription business. Creators must hold an eligible subscription to apply, and paid audience accounts determine which impressions can earn. The creator still carries the production risk.</p><p>The <a href="https://legal.x.com/en/original-content-rewards-terms.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">program terms</a> do not publish a fixed rate per thousand qualified impressions. They say payments are based on Premium-user impressions and allow X to change the calculation, add or remove revenue types, alter payout cadence or cancel the program. A creator can see the eligibility rules without being able to forecast revenue from them.</p><div class="techi-banner-block mx-auto my-8 w-full col-start-2 mb-4" data-no-article-callout="true"><div class="flex items-start gap-3 rounded-[14px] border px-5 py-4 shadow-[0_14px_34px_-26px_rgba(15,23,42,0.35)] border-amber-300/60 bg-amber-50/80 text-amber-950 dark:border-amber-400/25 dark:bg-amber-500/10 dark:text-amber-100"><span class="mt-0.5 inline-flex h-8 w-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-[10px] bg-amber-500/10 text-amber-700 dark:bg-amber-400/15 dark:text-amber-200"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-triangle-alert h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m21.73 18-8-14a2 2 0 0 0-3.48 0l-8 14A2 2 0 0 0 4 21h16a2 2 0 0 0 1.73-3"></path><path d="M12 9v4"></path><path d="M12 17h.01"></path></svg></span><div class="min-w-0 flex-1 [&amp;_p]:m-0 [&amp;_p]:text-[0.95rem] [&amp;_p]:leading-relaxed"><div class="payload-richtext max-w-none"><p>Creator payout note: X does not publish a fixed rate, can change its calculation, and does not guarantee admission or earnings. Creators should use the official terms and professional advice for legal or tax questions.</p></div></div></div></div><p>That uncertainty is not unusual in platform monetization, but it becomes more consequential when originality is an eligibility gate judged by the platform. Meta, for example, has also extended payouts across more formats through <a href="/facebook-new-story-monetization-feature/">monetization of Facebook Stories</a>. X&#x27;s sharper choice is to combine an originality review with a subscriber-weighted impression pool.</p><h2>The difficult part is enforcement, not the definition</h2><p>The new rules give X several ways to exclude content beyond straightforward copying. Posts created or published through automated means are ineligible. So are misleading content, posts with a helpful Community Note, material focused exclusively on monetization coaching, and repeated instructions asking users to like, repost, bookmark, follow or reply.</p><p>Some of those cases can be detected with strong evidence. Exact reuploads and artificial traffic leave measurable signals. “Meaningful” transformation and “genuine” perspective require judgment. Two accounts may cite the same public event, use the same source material and reach opposite eligibility decisions depending on how X evaluates their contribution.</p><p>X tried an earlier version of this incentive shift before replacing the program. In April, the company reduced payments to accounts it considered aggregators and penalized formulaic clickbait, according to <a href="https://www.theguardian.com/technology/2026/apr/13/x-cuts-payments-users-post-clickbait-recycle-news" target="_blank" rel="noopener">reporting on those creator payout cuts</a>. It also ran a writing contest framed around original Articles and verified Home Timeline impressions. The <a href="https://business.x.com/en/blog/creator-updates-and-article-contest-replacement" target="_blank" rel="noopener">official creator update</a> shows that long-form originality and paid-audience exposure were already moving toward the center of X&#x27;s strategy.</p><p>The replacement formalizes that direction, but formal rules do not remove appeals or edge cases. Rejected applicants get one appeal and can reapply after 90 days if the appeal fails. Current members whose monetization is paused for a previous violation cannot enroll at launch. X also reserves broad discretion to reject applications and remove accounts.</p><p>For creators, the quality of that review process will matter as much as the policy language. A program that cannot explain why one reaction is analysis and another is aggregation risks swapping engagement gaming for classification gaming. Accounts will learn to mimic whatever presentation survives review, even if the underlying contribution barely changes.</p><h2>What creators should check before September 7</h2><p>The transition is short enough that existing participants should treat it as an operational deadline, not a future policy note.</p><ul class="list-bullet"><li class="" value="1">Separate work you created from licensed material, reposts and third-party clips. Keep source files and permission records where ownership may be questioned.</li><li class="" value="2">Review Home Timeline impressions from verified users rather than relying on the largest impression number in analytics. Reply-driven accounts face a different threshold under the new program.</li><li class="" value="3">Make the added value visible. Reporting, analysis, context and creative editing should be identifiable without an evaluator having to infer the contribution.</li><li class="" value="4">Do not assume current enrollment transfers. Existing members must apply to Original Content Rewards when access opens.</li><li class="" value="5">Save old-program payout and analytics records before the September 7 retirement and reconcile the final payment expected around September 11.</li></ul><p>Creators have seen platforms revise a measurement rule after publishers built routines around it. A similar power imbalance surfaced when <a href="/spotify-podcast-play-count-update/">Spotify changed its podcast play-count plan after creator pushback</a>. X&#x27;s change reaches further because it can decide both whether the work counts and whether the audience viewing it counts.</p><h2>X is buying a different kind of supply</h2><p>Original Content Rewards is best read as a content-procurement policy. X wants reporting, commentary, images, video and analysis that people cannot obtain from a hundred interchangeable repost accounts. Paying creators is one way to attract that original supply to X instead of relying on copied artifacts from elsewhere.</p><p>The Premium filter reveals the commercial limit of that ambition. X is not assigning equal monetary value to every reader&#x27;s attention. It is using creator payouts to strengthen the experience of the subscribers most directly tied to revenue. That may reduce the economics of copy farms, but it may also steer creators toward topics and networks already dense with paid accounts.</p><p>Starting September 8, existing Revenue Sharing members will gradually learn whether they can apply. Longer-term confidence will depend on whether X makes originality decisions consistent, preserves legitimate commentary, and lets creators connect qualified attention to a payout they can understand. Until then, “only Premium views count” is the clearest rule in a program whose most important calculation remains private.</p></div><section aria-labelledby="post-faqs-heading" class="techi-post-faqs not-prose mt-12 overflow-hidden rounded-[14px] border border-slate-200 bg-gradient-to-br from-slate-50 to-white shadow-[0_8px_24px_-18px_rgba(15,23,42,0.15)] dark:border-white/10 dark:from-white/[0.03] dark:to-transparent "><div class="relative border-b border-slate-200 px-5 py-4 dark:border-white/10"><span class="absolute inset-y-0 left-0 w-[3px] bg-techi-teal" Applicants must meet the new program&#x27;s eligibility requirements.</p></div></details><details class="group bg-white px-5 py-4 transition-colors open:bg-slate-50/60 dark:bg-transparent dark:open:bg-white/[0.03]"><summary class="flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start gap-3 text-[15px] font-bold leading-snug text-slate-900 hover:text-techi-teal-press dark:text-white dark:hover:text-techi-cyan"><span class="mt-0.5 inline-flex h-6 w-6 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-slate-300 text-sm font-bold text-slate-500 transition-colors group-open:border-techi-teal group-open:bg-techi-teal group-open:text-white dark:border-white/15 dark:text-slate-400" aria-hidden="true"><svg class="h-3.5 w-3.5 transition-transform duration-200 group-open:rotate-45" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"><path d="M12 5v14M5 12h14"></path></svg></span><span class="flex-1">What counts as original content on X?</span></summary><div class="techi-post-faqs__answer mt-3 pl-9 text-[14.5px] leading-relaxed text-slate-700 dark:text-slate-300"><p class="mt-2 first:mt-0">X includes work a creator personally writes, films, designs or produces, plus commentary or analysis that adds genuine perspective. Copied, minimally modified, aggregated and cross-platform reposts by anyone other than the original creator do not qualify.</p></div></details><details class="group bg-white px-5 py-4 transition-colors open:bg-slate-50/60 dark:bg-transparent dark:open:bg-white/[0.03]"><summary class="flex cursor-pointer list-none items-start gap-3 text-[15px] font-bold leading-snug text-slate-900 hover:text-techi-teal-press dark:text-white dark:hover:text-techi-cyan"><span class="mt-0.5 inline-flex h-6 w-6 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-slate-300 text-sm font-bold text-slate-500 transition-colors group-open:border-techi-teal group-open:bg-techi-teal group-open:text-white dark:border-white/15 dark:text-slate-400" aria-hidden="true"><svg class="h-3.5 w-3.5 transition-transform duration-200 group-open:rotate-45" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"><path d="M12 5v14M5 12h14"></path></svg></span><span class="flex-1">Which views count toward X Original Content Rewards payouts?</span></summary><div class="techi-post-faqs__answer mt-3 pl-9 text-[14.5px] leading-relaxed text-slate-700 dark:text-slate-300"><p class="mt-2 first:mt-0">Qualified payout impressions are unique Home Timeline views from X Premium users where at least 50% of the post is visible. Paid, promoted, fraudulent and artificially generated impressions are excluded.</p></div></details></div></section></div><div class="mt-4 flex items-center gap-3 pt-2"></div><nav aria-label="Tags" application, not a renamed account setting\"]}]\n4e:[\"$\",\"p\",\"4\",{\"children\":[\"Current Revenue Sharing members do not roll over automatically. X says they will keep earning through September 7 and receive scheduled payouts on August 14 and August 28, followed by a final payment for eligible earnings accrued through the retirement date, expected around September 11. The \",[\"$\",\"a\",\"1\",{\"href\":\"https://help.x.com/en/using-x/creator-revenue-sharing\",\"target\":\"_blank\",\"rel\":\"noopener\",\"children\":[\"retirement notice\"]}],\" directs those creators to apply for the new program when access begins rolling out.\"]}]\n4f:[\"$\",\"p\",\"5\",{\"children\":[\"The replacement also changes the headline entry metric. Applicants need at least 500,000 Home Timeline impressions from verified users during the previous 90 days, at least 500 verified followers, an active Premium, Premium+ or Premium Business subscription, and an account in good standing. They must also be at least 18, live in a supported country, and apply through an eligible personal or business account; accounts belonging to political or government organizations are excluded. Replies do not count toward the 500,000-impression requirement. X says applications should receive a decision within three business days, but meeting the listed conditions does not guarantee admission.\"]}]\n50:[\"$\",\"p\",\"6\",{\"children\":[\"The old program required 5 million organic impressions over three months. It is tempting to describe 500,000 as a 90% cut in the bar, but the two numbers are not equivalent. The new threshold is tied to verified-user Home Timeline exposure and excludes reply impressions. A creator can have a much larger public reach and still miss the narrower qualifying metric.\"]}]\n51:[\"$\",\"p\",\"7\",{\"children\":[\"That distinction matters because X has spent years widening the formats creators can publish, including its \",[\"$\",\"$L13\",\"1\",{\"href\":\"/x-rolling-out-4k-video-feature-for-creators/\",\"children\":[\"4K video rollout for creators\"]}],\". Original Content Rewards says text posts, Articles, videos and images can all earn. Every major format is eligible, but payout credit still depends on distribution to a specific audience.\"]}]\n52:[\"$\",\"h2\",\"8\",{\"children\":[\"X drew a line between commentary and aggregation\"]}]\n53:[\"$\",\"p\",\"9\",{\"children\":[\"X's definition of originality is more practical than a blanket ban on using other people's material. Work that a creator writes, films, designs or produces qualifies. So can reaction, analysis and reporting that adds a genuine perspective or context. The policy recognizes that much of X's value comes from people interpreting events, not only uploading material created in isolation.\"]}]\n54:[\"$\",\"p\",\"10\",{\"children\":[\"The exclusions target a familiar set of high-volume tactics: copying a post, downloading and reuploading someone else's media, changing a few words, adding a filter or text overlay, and compiling other creators' work without substantial new framing. Cross-platform reposts are ineligible when the person posting them is not the original creator. X is telling clipping and aggregation accounts that speed and reach alone no longer create payable value.\"]}]\n55:[\"$\",\"p\",\"11\",{\"children\":[\"There is an important legal boundary in the policy. Original Content Rewards is an eligibility test, not a copyright license. X says a transformed post could satisfy the rewards definition and still infringe intellectual property rights. The creator remains responsible for permissions. In other words, “original enough to earn” and “lawful to publish” are separate judgments.\"]}]\n56:[\"$\",\"p\",\"12\",{\"children\":[\"The move fits a wider effort to extract more revenue and distinct content from the same network. \",[\"$\",\"$L13\",\"1\",{\"href\":\"/x-launches-marketplace-inactive-usernames/\",\"children\":[\"X's marketplace for inactive usernames\"]}],\" monetized scarce identity. Original Content Rewards applies a similar scarcity logic to posts: copied supply is abundant, while material associated with a recognizable voice is harder to replace.\"]}]"])</script><script nonce="KIBJERdON327V9lNNBG6EA==">self.__next_f.push([1,"\n57:[\"$\",\"h2\",\"13\",{\"children\":[\"Premium impressions are the economic center\"]}]\n58:[\"$\",\"p\",\"14\",{\"children\":[\"For payouts, X defines a qualified impression as a unique view from a Premium Basic, Premium, Premium+ or Premium Business user in the Home Timeline, with at least half of the post visible. Repeat views from the same account on the same post do not add another qualified impression. Paid, promoted, fraudulent and artificially generated views are excluded.\"]}]\n59:[\"$\",\"p\",\"15\",{\"children\":[\"This means a creator's total view counter and payable audience can diverge sharply. A post can travel among non-paying users, appear in replies or circulate through routes outside the Home Timeline without those views becoming qualified impressions. Public reach may still help a post spread, but X has reserved direct payout value for attention coming from subscribers.\"]}]\n5a:[\"$\",\"p\",\"16\",{\"children\":[\"That arrangement aligns the program with X's subscription business. Creators must hold an eligible subscription to apply, and paid audience accounts determine which impressions can earn.
